He finally agreed to seek psychiatric help after confiding to a fellow physician that he was convinced his patients would be better off without him and had started to dwell repeatedly on the idea that There are very rare days when my mood lifts slightly, and during those brief moments, the voices and intense guilt fade into the background. I just feel so tired and empty all the time.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if these feelings of emptiness and thoughts about not wanting to wake up have been constant over the past year, or have they come and gone in episodes?\n\nPatient: They have been completely constant, day in and day out. Ever since my dad passed away, there hasn't been a break or any episodes where I felt better. It just feels like a heavy, continuous weight that never lifts.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood or a few weeks where you felt relatively normal since your father passed away, or has it been a persistent low mood the entire time?\n\nPatient: It has been a persistent low mood the entire time, without any weeks of feeling normal. At most, there are only rare, brief days where my mood lifts slightly, and during those moments the heavy guilt and the voices fade into the background. Otherwise, it has just been a constant, steady struggle for over a year.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned a persistent low mood for over a year without significant improvement, but can you clarify if there have been any distinct periods or episodes where your symptoms worsened or improved slightly, even if briefly, and what might have triggered those changes?\n\nPatient: The low mood has been mostly steady and continuous for a little over a year since my father passed away, without any real periods of recovery. On the rare days when my mood lifts even slightly, the intense feelings of blame and hearing my father's voice do fade into the background. Otherwise, I haven't noticed distinct episodes, just a constant weight that stays with me.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent low mood for over a year, but can you tell me if there have been any periods, even brief, where you felt nearly back to your normal self, or has it been a completely unbroken stretch of feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It has been a completely unbroken stretch, and I haven't felt truly back to my normal self at any point over this past year. There are very rare days when my mood lifts slightly, and during those brief moments, the voices and intense guilt fade into the background. But even then, I never feel like my old self—it is just a steady, exhausting struggle.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans or taken any steps towards acting on these thoughts of not wanting to wake up or feeling like everyone would be better off without you?\n\nPatient: I haven't taken any active steps or made concrete plans to do anything. But on my worst days, I do catch myself picturing specific ways to end my life, like driving my car into a highway barrier on my commute. It just feels like it would be such a relief to finally stop fighting this.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned a constant low mood for over a year, but can you tell me if you've experienced any periods of significant depressive episodes where your symptoms were worse than your current state, or has it been this steady, persistent low mood the entire time?\n\nPatient: It has been this steady, persistent low mood the entire time rather than coming in separate, worse episodes. Since my father's death, it has just been one continuous, heavy struggle that has remained completely constant. There haven't been distinct shifts; it's just been this same exhausting weight every single day.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm whether you have experienced any clear episodes of depression that lasted for a shorter period, say a few weeks or months, followed by periods where your mood was significantly better, or has it been one long, continuous period of low mood since your father passed away?\n\nPatient: It has been one long, continuous struggle with low mood for a little over a year since my father passed away, rather than separate, short-lived episodes. While there are rare days when my mood briefly lifts a bit and the voices and intense guilt fade slightly, the depression itself has been steady and ongoing without any real periods of recovery.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, you've had this continuous low mood for over a year without any distinct episodes of improvement or worsening, but can you tell me if there have been any changes in the intensity of your symptoms, such as periods where you felt even more hopeless or sad than others, even if the overall mood has remained low?\n\nPatient: Yes, the intensity of my symptoms does fluctuate. On my worst days, the desire to disappear turns into picturing specific ways to end my life, like driving my car into a highway barrier. On the other hand, on the rare days when my mood lifts slightly, the accusing voices of my father and the constant feeling that everyone blames me do fade into the background."

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.
